and cpu cgroups.\\n839: // On systemd environments, it uses the name=systemd cgroup for the specified pid.\\n840: func getContainer(logger klog.Logger, pid int) (string, error) {\\n841: \\tcgs, err := cgroups.ParseCgroupFile(fmt.Sprintf(\\\"/proc/%d/cgroup\\\", pid))\\n842: \\tif err != nil {\\n843: \\t\\treturn \\\"\\\", err\\n844: \\t}\\n845: \\n846: \\tif cgroups.IsCgroup2UnifiedMode() {\\n847: \\t\\tc, found := cgs[\\\"\\\"]\\n848: \\t\\tif !found {\\n849: \\t\\t\\treturn \\\"\\\", cgroups.NewNotFoundError(\\\"unified\\\")\\n850: \\t\\t}\\n851: \\t\\treturn c, nil\\n852: \\t}\\n853: \\n854: hierarchy.\\n864: \\tif cpu != memory {\\n865: \\t\\treturn \\\"\\\", fmt.Errorf(\\\"cpu and memory cgroup hierarchy not unified.  cpu: %s, memory: %s\\\", cpu, memory)\\n866: \\t}\\n867: \\n868: \\t// on systemd, every pid is in a unified cgroup hierarchy (name=systemd as seen in systemd-cgls)\\n869: \\t// cpu and memory accounting is off by default, users may choose to enable it per unit or globally.\\n870: \\t// users could enable CPU and memory accounting globally via /etc/systemd/system.conf (DefaultCPUAccounting=true DefaultMemoryAccounting=true).\\n871: \\t// users could also enable CPU and memory accounting per unit via CPUAccounting=true and MemoryAccounting=true\\n872: \\t// we only warn if accounting is not enabled for CPU or memory so as to not break local development flows where kubelet is launched in a terminal.\\n873: \\t// for example, the cgroup for the user session will be something like /user.slice/user-X.slice/session-X.scope, but the cpu and memory\\n874: \\t// cgroup will be the closest ancestor where accounting is performed (most likely /) on systems that launch docker containers.\\n875: \\t// as a result, on those systems, you will not get cpu or memory accounting statistics for kubelet.\\n876: \\t// in addition, you would not get memory or cpu accounting for the runtime unless accounting was enabled on its unit (or globally).\\n877: \\tif systemd, leaving kernel threads at root cgroup is that we don't want to tie the\\n894: // execution of these threads with to-be defined /system quota and create priority inversions.\\n895: func ensureSystemCgroups(logger klog.Logger, rootCgroupPath string, manager cgroups.Manager) error {\\n896: \\t// Move non-kernel PIDs to the system container.\\n897: \\t// Only keep errors on latest attempt.\\n898: \\tvar finalErr error\\n899: \\tfor i := 0; i <= 10; i++ {\\n900: \\t\\tallPids, err := cmutil.GetPids(rootCgroupPath)\\n901: \\t\\tif err != nil {\\n902: \\t\\t\\tfinalErr = kubelet containers)\\n907: \\t\\tpids := make([]int, 0, len(allPids))\\n908: \\t\\tfor _, pid := range allPids {\\n909: \\t\\t\\tif pid == 1 || isKernelPid(pid) {\\n910: \\t\\t\\t\\tcontinue\\n911: \\t}\\n834: \\treturn utilerrors.NewAggregate(errs)\\n835: }\\n836: \\n837: // getContainer returns the cgroup associated with the specified pid.\\n838: // It enforces a unified hierarchy for memory and cpu cgroups.\\n839: // On systemd environments, it uses the name=systemd cgroup for the specified pid.\\n840: func getContainer(logger klog.Logger, pid int) (string, error) {\\n841: \\tcgs, err := \\t// on systemd, every pid is in a unified cgroup hierarchy (name=systemd as seen in systemd-cgls)\\n869: \\t// cpu and memory accounting is off by default, users may choose to enable it per unit or globally.\\n870: \\t// users could enable CPU and memory accounting globally via /etc/systemd/system.conf (DefaultCPUAccounting=true DefaultMemoryAccounting=true).\\n871: \\t// users could also enable CPU and memory accounting per unit via CPUAccounting=true and MemoryAccounting=true\\n872: \\t// we only warn if accounting is not enabled for CPU or memory so as to not break local development flows where kubelet is launched in a terminal.\\n873: \\t// for example, the cgroup for the user session will be something like /user.slice/user-X.slice/session-X.scope, but the cpu and memory\\n874: \\t// cgroup will be the closest ancestor where accounting is performed (most likely /) on systems that launch docker containers.\\n875: \\t// as a result, on those systems, you will not get cpu or memory accounting statistics for kubelet.\\n876: \\t// in addition, you would not get memory or cpu accounting for the runtime unless accounting was enabled on its unit (or globally).\\n877: \\tif systemd, leaving kernel threads at root cgroup is that we don't want to tie the\\n894: // execution of these threads with to-be defined /system quota and create priority inversions.\\n895: func \\t\\t\\t}\\n933: \\t\\t}\\n934: \\n935: \\t}\\n936: \\n937: \\treturn finalErr\\n938: }\\n939: \\n940: // Determines whether the specified PID is a kernel PID.\\n941: func isKernelPid(pid int) bool // setupKernelTunables validates kernel tunable flags are set as expected\n457: // depending upon the specified option, it will either warn, error, or modify the kernel tunable flags\n458: func setupKernelTunables(logger klog.Logger, option KernelTunableBehavior) error {\n459: \tdesiredState := map[string]int{\n460: \t\tutilsysctl.VMOvercommitMemory: desiredState {\n472: \t\tval, err := sysctl.GetSysctl(flag)\n473: \t\tif err != nil {\n474: \t\t\terrList = append(errList, err)\n475: \t\t\tcontinue\n476: \t\t}\n477: \t\tif val == expectedValue {\n478: \t\t\tcontinue\n479: \t\t}\n480: \n481: \t\tswitch option {\n482: \t\tcase KernelTunableError:\n483: \t\t\terrList = append(errList, fmt.Errorf(\"invalid kernel flag: %v, expected value: %v, actual value: %v\", flag, expectedValue, val))\n484: \t\tcase KernelTunableWarn:\n485: \t\t\tlogger.V(2).Info(\"Invalid kernel flag\", \"flag\", flag, \"expectedValue\", expectedValue, \"actualValue\", val)\n486: \t\tcase KernelTunableModify:\n487: \t\t\tlogger.V(2).Info(\"Updating kernel flag\", \"flag\", flag, \"expectedValue\", expectedValue, \"actualValue\", val)\n488: \t\t\terr = sysctl.SetSysctl(flag, expectedValue)\n489: \t\t\tif err != nil {\n490: \t\t\t\tif inuserns.RunningInUserNS() {\n491: \t\t\t\t\tif utilfeature.DefaultFeatureGate.Enabled(kubefeatures.KubeletInUserNamespace) {\n492: \t\t\t\t\t\tlogger.V(2).Info(\"Updating kernel flag failed (running in UserNS, ignoring)\", \"flag\", flag, \"err\", err)\n493: \t\t\t\t\t\tcontinue\n494: \t\t\t\t\t}\n495: \t\t\t\t\tlogger.Error(err, \"Updating kernel flag failed (Hint: enable KubeletInUserNamespace feature flag to ignore the error)\", \"flag\", enforces a unified hierarchy for memory and cpu cgroups.\n839: // On systemd environments, it uses the name=systemd cgroup for the specified pid.\n840: func getContainer(logger klog.Logger, pid int) (string, error) {\n841: \tcgs, err := cgroups.ParseCgroupFile(fmt.Sprintf(\"/proc/%d/cgroup\", pid))\n842: \tif err != nil {\n843: \t\treturn \"\", err\n844: \t}\n845: \n846: \tif cgroups.IsCgroup2UnifiedMode() {\n847: \t\tc, found := cgs[\"\"]\n848: \t\tif !found {\n849: \t\t\treturn \"\", cgroups.NewNotFoundError(\"unified\")\n850: \t\t}\n851: \t\treturn c, nil\n852: \t}\n853: \n854: \tcpu, found := cgs[\"cpu\"]\n855: \tif !found {\n856: \t\treturn \"\", cgroups.NewNotFoundError(\"cpu\")\n857: \t}\n858: \tmemory, found := cgs[\"memory\"]\n859: \tif !found {\n860: \t\treturn \"\", cgroups.NewNotFoundError(\"memory\")\n861: \t}\n862: \n863: \t// since we use this container for accounting, we need to ensure its a unified hierarchy.\n864: \tif cpu != memory {\n865: \t\treturn \"\", fmt.Errorf(\"cpu and memory cgroup hierarchy not unified.  cpu: %s, memory: %s\", cpu, memory)\n866: \t}\n867: \n868: \t// on systemd, every pid is in a unified cgroup hierarchy (name=systemd as seen in systemd-cgls)\n869: \t// cpu and memory accounting is off by default, users may choose to enable it per unit or globally.\n870: \t// users could enable CPU and memory accounting globally via /etc/systemd/system.conf (DefaultCPUAccounting=true DefaultMemoryAccounting=true).\n871: \t// users could also enable CPU and memory accounting per unit via CPUAccounting=true and MemoryAccounting=true\n872: \t// we only warn if accounting is not enabled for CPU or memory so as to not break local development flows where kubelet is launched in a terminal.\n873: \t// for example, the cgroup for the user session will be something like /user.slice/user-X.slice/session-X.scope, but the cpu and memory\n874: \t// cgroup will be the closest ancestor where accounting is performed (most likely /) on systems that launch docker containers.\n875: \t// as a result, on those systems, you will not get cpu or memory accounting statistics for kubelet.\n876: \t// in addition, you would not get memory or cpu accounting for the runtime unless accounting was enabled on its unit (or globally).\n877: \tif systemd, found := cgs[\"name=systemd\"]; found {\n878: \t\tif systemd != cpu {\n879: \t\t\tlogger.Info(\"CPUAccounting not enabled for process\", \"pid\", pid)\n880: \t\t}\n881: \t\tif systemd != memory {\n882: \t\t\tlogger.Info(\"MemoryAccounting not enabled for process\", \"pid\", pid)\n883: \t\t}\n884: \t\treturn systemd, nil\n885: \t}\n886: \n887: \treturn cpu, nil\n888: }\n889: \n890: // Ensures the system container is created and all non-kernel threads and process 1\n891: // without a container are moved to it.\n892: //\n893: // The reason of leaving kernel threads at root cgroup is that we don't want to tie the\n894: // execution of these threads with to-be defined /system quota and create priority inversions.\n895: func ensureSystemCgroups(logger klog.Logger, rootCgroupPath string, manager cgroups.Manager) error {\n896: \t// Move non-kernel PIDs to the system container.\n897: \t// Only keep errors on latest attempt.\n898: \tvar finalErr error\n899: \tfor i := 0; i <= 10; i++ {\n900: \t\tallPids, err := cmutil.GetPids(rootCgroupPath)\n901: \t\tif err != nil {\n902: \t\t\tfinalErr = fmt.Errorf(\"failed to list PIDs for root: %v\", err)\n903: \t\t\tcontinue\n904: \t\t}\n905: {\n909: \t\t\tif pid == 1 || isKernelPid(pid) {\n910: \t\t\t\tcontinue\n911: \t\t\t}\n912: \n913: \t\t\tpids = append(pids, pid)\n914: \t\t}\n915: \n916: \t\t// Check if we have moved all the non-kernel PIDs.\n917: \t\tif len(pids) == 0 {\n918: \t\t\treturn nil\n919: \t\t}\n920: \n921: \t\tlogger.V(3).Info(\"Moving non-kernel processes\", \"pids\", pids)\n922: \t\tfor _, pid := range pids {\n923: \t\t\terr := manager.Apply(pid)\n924: \t\t\tif err != nil {\n925: \t\t\t\tname := \"\"\n926: \t\t\t\tcgroups, err := manager.GetCgroups()\n927: \t\t\t\tif err == nil {\n928: \t\t\t\t\tname = \n937: \treturn finalErr\n938: }\n939: \n940: // Determines whether the specified PID is a kernel PID.\n941: func isKernelPid(pid int) bool {\n942: \t// Kernel threads have no associated // getQoSMemoryRequests sums and returns the memory request of all pods for\\n206: // guaranteed and burstable qos classes.\\n207: func (m *qosContainerManagerImpl) getQoSMemoryRequests() *qosContainerManagerImpl) setMemoryReserve(logger klog.Logger, configs map[v1.PodQOSClass]*CgroupConfig, percentReserve int64) {\\n237: \\tqosMemoryRequests := m.getQoSMemoryRequests()\\n238: \\n239: be set in the first Update() and adjusts\\n264: // their memory limit to the usage to prevent further growth.\\n265: func (m *qosContainerManagerImpl) retrySetMemoryReserve(logger klog.Logger, configs \\t// In the case of a None policy, cgroupv2 and systemd cgroup manager, we must make sure systemd is aware of the cpuset cgroup.\\n191: \\t// By default, systemd will not create it, as we've not chosen to delegate it, and we haven't included it in the Apply() request.\\n192: \\t// However, this causes a bug where kubelet restarts unnecessarily (cpuset cgroup is created in the cgroupfs, but systemd\\n193: \\t// doesn't know about it and deletes it, and then kubelet doesn't continue because the cgroup isn't configured as expected).\\n194: \\t// An alternative is to delegate the `cpuset` cgroup to the kubelet, but that would require some plumbing in libcontainer,\\n195: \\t// and this is sufficient.\\n196: \\t// Only do so on None policy, as Static policy will do its own updating of \\n252: func (cm *containerManagerImpl) getNodeAllocatableAbsoluteImpl(capacity v1.ResourceList) v1.ResourceList {\\n253: \\tresult := make(v1.ResourceList)\\n254: \\tfor k, v := range capacity where kubelet restarts unnecessarily (cpuset cgroup is created in the cgroupfs, but systemd\n193: \t// doesn't know about it and deletes it, and then kubelet doesn't continue because the cgroup isn't \t// Only do so on None policy, as Static policy will do its own updating of the cpuset.\n197: \t// Please see the comment on policy none's GetAllocatableCPUs\n198: \tif cm.cpuManager.GetAllocatableCPUs().IsEmpty() {\n199: \t\trc.CPUSet = cm.cpuManager.GetAllCPUs()\n200: \t}\n201: \n202: \treturn rc\n203: }\n204: \n205: // getCgroupConfigInternal are the pieces of getCgroupConfig that don't require the cm object.\n206: // This is added to unit test without needing to create a full containerManager\n207: func getCgroupConfigInternal(rl v1.ResourceList, compressibleResourcesOnly bool) *ResourceConfig {\n208: \t// TODO(vishh): Set CPU Quota if necessary.\n209: \tif rl == nil {\n210: \t\treturn nil\n211: \t}\n212: \tvar rc ResourceConfig\n213: \n214: \tsetCompressibleResources := func() {\n215: \t\tif q, exists := rl[v1.ResourceCPU]; exists {\n216: \t\t\t// CPU is defined in milli-cores.\n217: \t\t\tval := MilliCPUToShares(q.MilliValue())\n218: \t\t\trc.CPUShares = &val\n219: \t\t}\n220: \t}\n221: \n222: \t// Only return compressible resources\n223: \tif compressibleResourcesOnly {\n224: \t\tsetCompressibleResources()\n225: \t} else {\n226: \t\tif q, exists := rl[v1.ResourceMemory]; exists {\n227: \t\t\t// Memory is defined in bytes.\n228: \t\t\tval := q.Value()\n229: \t\t\trc.Memory = &val\n230: \t\t}\n231: \n232: \t\tsetCompressibleResources()\n233: \n234: \t\tif q, exists := rl[pidlimit.PIDs]; exists {\n235: \t\t\tval := q.Value()\n236: \t\t\trc.PidsLimit = &val\n237: \t\t}\n238: \t\trc.HugePageLimit = HugePageLimits(rl)\n239: \t}\n240: \treturn &rc\n241: }\n242: \n243: // GetNodeAllocatableAbsolute returns the absolute value of Node Allocatable which is primarily useful for enforcement.\n244: // Note that not all resources that are available on the node are included in the returned list of resources.\n245: // Returns a ResourceList.\n246: func (cm *containerManagerImpl) GetNodeAllocatableAbsolute() v1.ResourceList {\n247: \tcm.RLock()\n248: \tdefer cm.RUnlock()\n249: \treturn cm.getNodeAllocatableAbsoluteImpl(cm.capacity)\n250: }\n251: \n252: func (cm *containerManagerImpl) getNodeAllocatableAbsoluteImpl(capacity v1.ResourceList) v1.ResourceList {\n253: \tresult := make(v1.ResourceList)\n254: \tfor k, v := range capacity {\n255: \t\tvalue := v.DeepCopy()\n256: \t\tif cm.NodeConfig.SystemReserved != nil {\n257: \t\t\tvalue.Sub(cm.NodeConfig.SystemReserved[k])\n258: \t\t}\n259: \t\tif cm.NodeConfig.KubeReserved != nil {\n260: \t\t\tvalue.Sub(cm.NodeConfig.KubeReserved[k])\n261: \t\t}\n262: \t\tif value.Sign() < 0 {\n263: \t\t\t// Negative Allocatable resources don't make sense.\n264: \t\t\tvalue.Set(0)\n265: \t\t}\n266: \t\tresult[k] = value\n267: \t}\n268: \treturn result\n269: }\n270: \n271: // getNodeAllocatableInternalAbsolute is similar to getNodeAllocatableAbsolute except that\n272: // it also includes internal resources (currently process IDs).  It is intended for setting\n273: // up top level cgroups only.\n274: func (cm *containerManagerImpl) getNodeAllocatableInternalAbsolute() v1.ResourceList {\n275: \treturn cm.getNodeAllocatableAbsoluteImpl(cm.internalCapacity)\n276: }\n277: \n278: // GetNodeAllocatableReservation returns amount of compute or storage resource that have to be reserved on this node from scheduling.\n279: func (cm *containerManagerImpl) GetNodeAllocatableReservation() v1.ResourceList {\n280: \tcm.RLock()\n281: \tdefer cm.RUnlock()\n282: \tevictionReservation := hardEvictionReservation(cm.HardEvictionThresholds, cm.capacity)\n283: \tresult := make(v1.ResourceList)\n284: \tfor k := range cm.capacity {\n285: \t\tvalue := resource.NewQuantity(0, resource.DecimalSI)\n286: \t\tif cm.NodeConfig.SystemReserved != nil {\n287: \t\t\tvalue.Add(cm.NodeConfig.SystemReserved[k])\n288: \t\t}\n289: \t\tif cm.NodeConfig.KubeReserved != nil {\n290: \t\t\tvalue.Add(cm.NodeConfig.KubeReserved[k])\n291: \t\t}\n292: \t\tif evictionReservation != nil {\n293: \t\t\tvalue.Add(evictionReservation[k])\n294: \t\t}\n295: \t\tif !value.IsZero() {\n296: \t\t\tresult[k] = *value\n297: \t\t}\n298: \t}\n299: \treturn result\n300: }\n301: \n302: // validateNodeAllocatable ensures that the user specified Node Allocatable Configuration doesn't reserve more than the node capacity.\n303: // Returns error if the configuration is invalid, nil otherwise.\n304: func (cm *containerManagerImpl) validateNodeAllocatable() error {\n305: \tcm.RLock()\n306: \tdefer cm.RUnlock()\n307: \tvar errors []string\n308: \tnar := cm.GetNodeAllocatableReservation()\n309: \tfor k, v := range nar {\n310: \t\tvalue := cm.capacity[k].DeepCopy()\n311: \t\tvalue.Sub(v)\n312: \n313: \t\tif value.Sign() < 0 {\n314: \t\t\terrors = append(errors, fmt.Sprintf(\"Resource %q has a reservation of %v but capacity of %v. Expected capacity >= reservation.\", k, v, cm.capacity[k]))\n315: \t\t}\n316: \t}\n317: \n318: \tif len(errors) > 0 {\n319: \t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"invalid Node Allocatable configuration. %s\", strings.Join(errors, \" \"))\n320: \t}\n321: \treturn nil\n322: }\n323: \n324: // Using ObjectReference for events as the node maybe not cached; refer to #42701 for detail.\n325: func nodeRefFromNode(nodeName string) *v1.ObjectReference {\n326: \treturn &v1.ObjectReference{\n327: \t\tAPIVersion: \"v1\",\n328: \t\tKind:       \"Node\",\n329: \t\tName:       nodeName,\n330: \t\tNamespace:  \"\",\n331: \t}\n332: }\n333: ","url":"https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/github.com/kubernetes/kubernetes@b08fa0cdf18610be74d9741cf361446a5f42ff76/-/blob/pkg/kubelet/cm/node_container_manager_linux.go"}}}

As such, if more NUMA Nodes than this are\n39: \t// present on a machine and the TopologyManager is enabled, an error will\n40: \t// be returned and the TopologyManager will not be loaded.\n41: \tdefaultMaxAllowableNUMANodes = 8\n42: \t// ErrorTopologyAffinity represents the type for a TopologyAffinityError\n43: \tErrorTopologyAffinity = \"TopologyAffinityError\"\n44: )\n45: \n46: // TopologyAffinityError represents an resource alignment error\n47: type TopologyAffinityError struct{}\n48: \n49: func (e TopologyAffinityError) Error() string {\n50: \treturn \"Resources cannot be allocated with Topology locality\"\n51: }\n52: \n53: func (e TopologyAffinityError) Type() string {\n54: \treturn ErrorTopologyAffinity\n55: }\n56: \n57: // Manager interface provides methods for Kubelet to manage pod topology hints\n58: type Manager interface {\n59: \t// PodAdmitHandler is implemented by Manager\n60: \tlifecycle.PodAdmitHandler\n61: \t// AddHintProvider adds a hint provider to manager to indicate the hint provider\n62: \t// wants to be consulted with when making topology hints\n63: \tAddHintProvider(logger klog.Logger, h HintProvider)\n64: \t// AddContainer adds pod to Manager for tracking\n65: \tAddContainer(pod *v1.Pod, container *v1.Container, containerID string)\n66: \t// RemoveContainer removes pod from Manager tracking\n67: \tRemoveContainer(containerID string) error\n68: \t// Store is the interface for storing pod topology hints\n69: \tStore\n70: }\n71: \n72: type manager struct {\n73: \t//Topology Manager Scope\n74: \tscope Scope\n75: }\n76: \n77: // HintProvider is an interface for components that want to collaborate to\n78: // achieve globally optimal concrete resource alignment with respect to\n79: // NUMA locality.\n80: type HintProvider interface {\n81: \t// GetTopologyHints returns a map of resource names to a list of possible\n82: \t// concrete resource allocations in terms of NUMA locality hints. Each hint\n83: \t// is optionally marked \"preferred\" and indicates the set of NUMA nodes\n84: \t// involved in the hypothetical allocation. The topology manager calls\n85: \t// this function for each hint provider, and merges the hints to produce\n86: \t// a consensus \"best\" hint. The hint providers may subsequently query the\n87: \t// topology manager to influence actual resource assignment.\n88: \tGetTopologyHints(pod *v1.Pod, container *v1.Container) map[string][]TopologyHint\n89: \t// GetPodTopologyHints returns a map of resource names to a list of possible\n90: \t// concrete resource allocations per Pod in terms of NUMA locality hints.\n91: \tGetPodTopologyHints(pod *v1.Pod) map[string][]TopologyHint\n92: \t// Allocate triggers resource allocation to occur on the HintProvider after\n93: \t// all hints have been gathered and the aggregated Hint is available via a\n94: \t// call to Store.GetAffinity().\n95: \tAllocate(pod *v1.Pod, container *v1.Container) error\n96: }\n97: \n98: // Store interface is to allow Hint Providers to retrieve pod affinity\n99: type Store interface {\n100: \tGetAffinity(podUID string, containerName string) TopologyHint\n101: \tGetPolicy() Policy\n102: }\n103: \n104: // TopologyHint is a struct containing the NUMANodeAffinity for a Container\n105: type TopologyHint struct {\n106: \tNUMANodeAffinity bitmask.BitMask\n107: \t// Preferred is set to true when the NUMANodeAffinity encodes a preferred\n108: \t// allocation for the Container. It is set to false otherwise.\n109: \tPreferred bool\n110: }\n111: \n112: // IsEqual checks if TopologyHint are equal\n113: func (th *TopologyHint) IsEqual(topologyHint TopologyHint) bool {\n114: \tif th.Preferred == topologyHint.Preferred {\n115: \t\tif th.NUMANodeAffinity == nil || topologyHint.NUMANodeAffinity == nil {\n116: \t\t\treturn th.NUMANodeAffinity == topologyHint.NUMANodeAffinity\n117: \t\t}\n118: \t\treturn th.NUMANodeAffinity.IsEqual(topologyHint.NUMANodeAffinity)\n119: \t}\n120: \treturn false\n121: ","url":"https://sourcegraph.sourcegraph.com/github.com/kubernetes/kubernetes@b08fa0cdf18610be74d9741cf361446a5f42ff76/-/blob/pkg/kubelet/cm/topologymanager/topology_manager.go%3FL1-120"}}}

Let me write the architecture guide. Here's what I know:\n\n## Kubelet Container Manager Subsystem Architecture\n\n### Overview\nThe container manager (`pkg/kubelet/cm`) is a node-level subsystem within the kubelet responsible for:\n1. Managing system-level cgroup hierarchy for resource isolation\n2. Coordinating resource allocation across CPU, memory, and device managers\n3. Enforcing node allocatable limits\n4. Bridging device plugin infrastructure with container runtime\n\n### Key Components\n\n1. **ContainerManager interface** (`pkg/kubelet/cm/container_manager.go`)\n   - The primary interface with 25+ methods, with platform-specific implementations for Linux and Windows, plus a stub for unsupported platforms\n\n2. **containerManagerImpl** (Linux, `pkg/kubelet/cm/container_manager_linux.go`)\n   - Central orchestrator that holds references to all sub-managers including cadvisor, cgroup manager, QoS container manager, device manager, CPU manager, memory manager, topology manager, and DRA manager\n\n3. **CgroupManager interface** (`pkg/kubelet/cm/types.go`)\n   - Handles the abstraction for cgroup operations across different cgroup versions\n\n, with separate implementations for cgroup v1 and v2, each supporting both cgroupfs and systemd drivers\n\n4. **QOSContainerManager** (`pkg/kubelet/cm/qos_container_manager_linux.go`)\n   - Manages the top-level cgroups for the three QoS classes (Guaranteed, Burstable, BestEffort) and runs a periodic reconciliation loop every minute\n\n5. **PodContainerManager** (`pkg/kubelet/cm/types.go`, `pod_container_manager_linux.go`)\n   - Created through a factory method on containerManagerImpl and manages per-pod cgroup hierarchies under the QoS structure, with a full implementation when CgroupsPerQOS is enabled and a no-op fallback otherwise\n\n6. **Node Allocatable enforcement** (`pkg/kubelet/cm/node_container_manager_linux.go`)\n   - Sets up the kubepods cgroup and enforces resource limits based on system-reserved, kube-reserved, and eviction thresholds\n\n7. **CPUManager** (`pkg/kubelet/cm/cpumanager/`)\n   - Offers two policies: none for no exclusive allocation, and static for assigning exclusive CPUs to Guaranteed pods with integer CPU requests, persisting state in a checkpoint file and periodically reconciling container resources through the CRI\n\n8. **MemoryManager** (`pkg/kubelet/cm/memorymanager/`)... InternalContainerLifecycle acts as the bridge between the container manager and the runtime, applying CPU and memory affinity settings before container creation, then registering the container with the various managers during startup and cleaning up on shutdown. Platform support varies significantly—Linux has the full feature set with cgroup hierarchies, QoS-level isolation, and all the advanced managers, while other platforms have more limited capabilities.\n\nNow I'm looking at how the container manager initializes and starts up, detecting cgroup subsystems and creating the various manager components, then validating system requirements and setting up node allocatable resources.\n\nDuring pod admission, the topology manager coordinates hints from CPU, memory, and device managers before allocation happens. When containers are created, the system applies CPU and NUMA memory affinity settings, retrieves device resources, and then registers the container with the relevant managers after it starts.\n\nNow looking at pod synchronization and cleanup: the kubelet creates pod-level cgroup managers and updates QoS tier cgroups, then during teardown it removes containers from the topology manager, frees up exclusive CPUs, and destroys the pod cgroup hierarchy.\n\nThe cgroup structure places Guaranteed pods directly under `kubepods`, while Burstable and BestEffort pods get their own subdirectories with pod UIDs and container IDs nested underneath.\n\nKubernetes resource requests and limits map to different cgroup v1 and v2 parameters—CPU shares become weights, quota becomes max limits, and memory requests translate to minimum guarantees in v2.\n\nNow I'm looking at the failure modes and design tradeoffs: cgroup initialization failures are fatal, node allocatable updates retry in the background, device plugins get a five-minute grace period, and corrupted CPUManager state prevents startup. The hierarchy-per-QOS approach adds overhead but enables better resource isolation, while static CPU allocation improves determinism at the cost of fragmenting available CPUs.","signature":"EqY8CkYICxgCKkA6Bu4NRlkStpy6eNichAuDObs4SLCgRl4eFrfn052DJedmUiAWNfgDeqiQEjEmeWVon+YI6Ka6RqjQtyGI1t/EEgxk8Sk+D+8mBOkK4qYaDNCCegc7KI4bbE6ooSIwUbWnFLSNGjDmrZaKEorIKVNVY+itlvaTcA4g18lYV8BxWdyaORs6dq3epny/kgwoKo07LO8TTwnJ3rLojp6fpklDqibot1IX1Gu77Y02+9PwXKpOKY57/MKzOkKj6b46mq6taQbyylpMVeIitWnLgeeevIKQyn1LQmVtIl8AplwnOHVqU3oQb4JtH7KH5kArI8FlrVL3avcC6qTBwd5TkJ6tYK8/QpKN0vSlajNGqlMw3SInUyLIv/a34fdbTcBBW/UE1eezBtmmhAIurzh0KnjHT6wSskT+BD2b9+o8AxcsnLsVu822HTedqcNsXHM1290lqy1Iiinh9SX8dEAV1mvQ7k0OOisvQ4KPQ65CkTO0wVczrJzYOCFwFMWD/ajqb0o+5uH8c4zxw8pcKb/KlQoZBjyy9Adi8kOeBLjZyAqUkwVDpseGddyzHK5a/6PNuhnXOvOi3Ve3+4VnMiUjQfr+wdAOzgH27tJpf9OharMPdyAOigaQOr81O4dd5V85REpWbtKCdSwyNuUKwRYvjSO6TSFmetJkTz7e6Cxj89VSl09gU87Hqkg50z++VroAcVhWeOnBHHGbwgfRUdMfPicp8+83Ow9dLmRlL1jIXoO2tOQegT/BlXcccfAQxgqnC0UCQCueZ1Q8wcbBFVjvNwypDrQoplkgA+ysnGGxBRClnbGL8aUD17S9fKC9LjXOPtkVXoXNFFJj+TI7s9HkajYO5xIWgD379xKTnldK9uFi12dzvSv2AI2ilRGY8BNYMQ/QG0hdNGd3RX0U4jBGUrjFftMvODTvxusdAfQK4TXxxMN9DjoXNlw40Dyi0xVz8jIYSc8nX2LYYCf5aWsRCKyKwZ6LhBCAvOdYD2lKODmVbKACvshxHXsO0GuPiYv+Nv97zaNQw/VU/nw25gKBmGH97ZbaoadT+etozE5/j1tc7MOYhaBtg3AFTT7XBP0R7Zhr8dQULuzlT04f74oj5hQzgJ5mvOmD9/SJc4pHHbmS8xiXdocrbQG96N0sZl4idjxIWhAaNnf3jByMvtWK/RVA/zdnAf+rCB3Lp1/mubd6DtgvcdynrxJg7hh9IsRwkF7GoZhjhOxFgHIMVSpHommo/b9M0zH0uuRrhAXX5/WnSNicTd8mxGOBOrW+M/kWJ/A3NCwaKJbEIPa/Jv5/W1BlOrZ905SbwbyUSmMabbOuiFz93e2wgLcCcC6c8OuBaGYCKXAaaylJt8KCBzLaTRhc/Bb+luhpA+50tjfUYNj2Oc9VjIfUmK0ICp6v89VD7GxSP1rFVFKmsaNJHCowE11Eg5n6Mh7GFqE+yy/gXLYMeeA31GKFpHDXROEhr4VuxCnv5vNhl8XjqFPRDyeq/2rzqrvoSlhyB1JLVkT88anKWs2+lArn0aqGAal/XgKslx4f8N69NW1osz8CnUKsmruH+4qNCJMw96C6EEyLlsOSv1mbobD3Ml9J+4xYAH6aRoDb9bN2lt6K1C/ECL2hUj9LbSd616Zba1AzgQzDVICHByuhkaPTwYGqYop1WxDc2tbxA3n+HK0gwJWJY3i3pZ8LbQ+vVtxj0A7rBe0VimiekXtMyr0Hrpa/l8Pd4hRVbLqQSmB1euwVCyEwXRtDblMK76ha6BO7RyTT+VhG4YJaKgbh1BiQAWTlo+bOjxFUgmSas+GjwNMQTYtMbCAuGOZ8rJMHoMA0kivlXcewlJrQoSDtefVrFZIZ6Zpa1hXNCtmnh0/Cry0DdvlQKQshulan16M7wJ4m0EF62+tluo8Ojx3RJXVVoKlF5MQq77HHWy/FmghP0CG+C54EXSufGWrOt12jvxnrRIuNyUvx70giKy9g {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-4-6","id":"msg_018QVKtsF8YsenSXCJhNLBuw","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01XdzFk8xZv2ihAKPk6e3Wtg","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/workspace/documentation.md","content":"# Kubelet Container Manager: Subsystem Architecture Guide\n\n## 1. Purpose, Boundaries, and Dependencies\n\nThe kubelet container manager (`pkg/kubelet/cm`) is a node-level subsystem that sits between the kubelet's pod-sync machinery and the Linux kernel's resource control primitives. Its responsibilities are:\n\n- **Cgroup lifecycle management**: create, configure, and destroy cgroup hierarchies that enforce CPU, memory, pid, and hugepage limits per node, QoS class, and pod.\n- **Resource allocation coordination**: before a container starts, determine which CPUs, memory NUMA nodes, and devices it may use, and embed those decisions in the CRI container config.\n- **Node-level capacity accounting**: compute what is schedulable by subtracting system-reserved, kube-reserved, and eviction-threshold reservations from raw node capacity.\n- **Device plugin and DRA brokering**: register device plugins, allocate extended resources, and surface runtime mount/env/device options to the container runtime.\n- **Topology-aware placement**: coordinate NUMA-local resource allocation across CPU, memory, and device managers.\n\n**Upstream callers:** The kubelet main struct (`kubelet.go`) holds a single `cm.ContainerManager` reference. The kubelet calls into the container manager during pod admission (`GetAllocateResourcesPodAdmitHandler`), pod sync (`NewPodContainerManager`, `UpdateQOSCgroups`, `EnsureExists`), and container creation (`GetResources`, `InternalContainerLifecycle`).\n\n**Downstream:** The container manager drives the Linux kernel through the `opencontainers/cgroups` library (cgroupfs or systemd paths) and calls back into the CRI runtime via `UpdateContainerResources` for CPU reconciliation. It does not directly write container image layers or network namespaces—those belong to separate kubelet subsystems.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Key Components and Responsibility Split\n\n### 2.1 `ContainerManager` Interface\n**File:** `pkg/kubelet/cm/container_manager.go:66`\n\nThe 25-method interface is the only type the kubelet interacts with. It includes methods for:\n- Starting sub-managers and node cgroup setup (`Start`).\n- Capacity/allocatability queries (`GetCapacity`, `GetNodeAllocatableReservation`, `GetNodeAllocatableAbsolute`).\n- QoS cgroup housekeeping (`UpdateQOSCgroups`, `NewPodContainerManager`).\n- Extended resource allocation (`GetResources`, `UpdatePluginResources`, `PrepareDynamicResources`).\n- Lifecycle hooks (`InternalContainerLifecycle`).\n- PodResources API surface (`GetDevices`, `GetCPUs`, `GetMemory`, `GetDynamicResources`).\n\nThere are three platform implementations: `containerManagerImpl` for Linux, a separate `containerManagerImpl` for Windows, and a stub for unsupported OS targets.\n\n---\n\n### 2.2 `containerManagerImpl` (Linux)\n**File:** `pkg/kubelet/cm/container_manager_linux.go:100`\n\nThe Linux implementation is the orchestrator. Its struct fields directly reveal the ownership graph:\n\n```go\ntype containerManagerImpl struct {\n    cadvisorInterface   cadvisor.Interface        // hardware topology + capacity\n    cgroupManager       CgroupManager             // kernel cgroup CRUD\n    qosContainerManager QOSContainerManager       // burstable/besteffort tiers\n    deviceManager       devicemanager.Manager     // device plugin broker\n    cpuManager          cpumanager.Manager        // exclusive CPU allocation\n    memoryManager       memorymanager.Manager     // NUMA memory affinity\n    topologyManager     topologymanager.Manager   // cross-resource NUMA hint arbiter\n    draManager          *dra.Manager              // Dynamic Resource Allocation\n    systemContainers    []*systemContainer        // kubelet + system process cgroups\n    resourceUpdates     chan resourceupdates.Update\n}\n```\n\nThe `resourceUpdates` channel fans in updates from `deviceManager` and `draManager` into a single stream consumed by the kubelet's main sync loop, so that device health changes can trigger pod status reconciliation.\n\n---\n\n### 2.3 `CgroupManager`\n**Files:** `pkg/kubelet/cm/types.go:64`, `pkg/kubelet/cm/cgroup_v1_manager_linux.go`, `pkg/kubelet/cm/cgroup_v2_manager_linux.go`\n\n`CgroupManager` abstracts all kernel cgroup operations behind eight methods: `Create`, `Destroy`, `Update`, `Validate`, `Exists`, `Name`, `Pids`, `ReduceCPULimits`, `MemoryUsage`, `GetCgroupConfig`, `SetCgroupConfig`, `Version`.\n\nThe `NewCgroupManager` factory (called from `NewContainerManager`) detects the cgroup version at startup and returns either `cgroupV1impl` or `cgroupV2impl`. Each implementation wraps `opencontainers/cgroups/manager` but handles v1/v2 file names differently (e.g., `memory.limit_in_bytes` vs `memory.max`, `cpu.shares` vs `cpu.weight`).\n\n`CgroupName` is an abstract slice of path components (`[]string`). The `Name()` method on `CgroupManager` converts that slice to the concrete filesystem path, applying systemd escaping rules if the cgroup driver is `systemd` (e.g., `{kubepods, burstable, pod<uid>}` → `/kubepods.slice/kubepods-burstable.slice/kubepods-burstable-pod<uid>.slice`). This translation layer is the only place that understands driver-specific naming.\n\n---\n\n### 2.4 QoS Cgroup Hierarchy\n**Files:** `pkg/kubelet/cm/qos_container_manager_linux.go`, `pkg/kubelet/cm/node_container_manager_linux.go`\n\nWhen `--cgroups-per-qos=true`, the container manager creates a three-tier cgroup tree:\n\n```\n/kubepods              ← Node Allocatable cgroup (defaultNodeAllocatableCgroupName)\n├── /kubepods/burstable     ← Burstable QoS\n│   └── /kubepods/burstable/pod<UID>   ← per-pod\n└── /kubepods/besteffort    ← BestEffort QoS\n    └── /kubepods/besteffort/pod<UID>\n(Guaranteed pods sit directly under /kubepods/pod<UID>)\n```\n\nThe `/kubepods` cgroup enforces Node Allocatable (node capacity minus system-reserved, kube-reserved, and eviction thresholds). Its CPU shares and memory limit are set in `enforceNodeAllocatableCgroups` (`node_container_manager_linux.go:67`). On an already-running node with pods, the kubelet retries enforcing this limit in a background goroutine because existing pod memory usage might temporarily exceed the new limit; enforcement is eventually consistent via eviction.\n\n`QOSContainerManager.UpdateCgroups` (`qos_container_manager_linux.go:316`) runs every minute. It recomputes:\n- `BestEffort` CPU shares: pinned to the kernel minimum (`MinShares = 2`).\n- `Burstable` CPU shares: sum of all Burstable pods' CPU requests, translated to kernel shares via `MilliCPUToShares`.\n- Memory limits for each tier (when `QOSReserved` feature is enabled), with a retry path that sets the limit to current usage if the first attempt fails due to existing usage exceeding the desired limit.\n- `memory.min` (cgroup v2 `MemoryQoS` feature gate): reserves the sum of pod requests at the QoS tier boundary, preventing lower-priority workloads from evicting Guaranteed pod memory.\n\n---\n\n### 2.5 `PodContainerManager`\n**Files:** `pkg/kubelet/cm/types.go:109`, `pkg/kubelet/cm/pod_container_manager_linux.go`\n\n`PodContainerManager` is a factory product of `ContainerManager.NewPodContainerManager()`. When `--cgroups-per-qos=true`, `NewPodContainerManager` returns `podContainerManagerImpl`; otherwise it returns `podContainerManagerNoop` (all operations are no-ops).\n\n`EnsureExists` (`pod_container_manager_linux.go:74`) creates the pod cgroup if it does not exist, using `ResourceConfigForPod` to translate `pod.Spec.Containers[*].Resources` into a `CgroupConfig`. If the `DisableCPUQuotaWithExclusiveCPUs` feature gate is on and the pod has exclusive CPUs (checked via `containerManager.PodHasExclusiveCPUs`), CPU CFS quota enforcement is disabled for that pod.\n\n`Destroy` first kills all processes currently charged to the pod cgroup via `tryKillingCgroupProcesses` (up to five retry attempts with individual pid kills), then calls `CgroupManager.Destroy`.\n\n`GetAllPodsFromCgroups` scans the cgroup filesystem under each QoS cgroup to find pod UIDs from directory names (e.g., `pod<UID>`), providing the orphan-cleanup mechanism used during kubelet startup.\n\n---\n\n### 2.6 `InternalContainerLifecycle`\n**Files:** `pkg/kubelet/cm/internal_container_lifecycle.go`, `pkg/kubelet/cm/internal_container_lifecycle_linux.go`\n\nThis interface (`PreCreateContainer`, `PreStartContainer`, `PostStopContainer`) is the narrow bridge between the container manager and the kuberuntime container creation path (`pkg/kubelet/kuberuntime`). Its Linux implementation (`internalContainerLifecycleImpl`) carries references to cpuManager, memoryManager, and topologyManager.\n\n- **`PreCreateContainer`** (Linux only, `internal_container_lifecycle_linux.go:31`): Reads `cpuManager.GetCPUAffinity()` and, if non-empty, writes the resulting `cpuset.CPUSet` into `containerConfig.Linux.Resources.CpusetCpus`. Reads `memoryManager.GetMemoryNUMANodes()` and writes NUMA node IDs into `containerConfig.Linux.Resources.CpusetMems`. This is the point where CPU/memory manager decisions become concrete CRI parameters.\n- **`PreStartContainer`** (`internal_container_lifecycle.go:41`): After the container is created (and its containerID is known), this call registers the container with cpuManager, memoryManager, and topologyManager. This binding is what allows the reconciliation loop to apply future resource updates.\n- **`PostStopContainer`** (`internal_container_lifecycle.go:55`): Removes the containerID from the topologyManager, which in turn allows the topologyManager to clean up cross-resource NUMA affinity tracking.\n\n---\n\n### 2.7 CPUManager\n**Files:** `pkg/kubelet/cm/cpumanager/cpu_manager.go`, `pkg/kubelet/cm/cpumanager/policy_static.go`, `pkg/kubelet/cm/cpumanager/policy_none.go`\n\nCPUManager exposes a `Manager` interface (`cpu_manager.go:56`) with `Allocate`, `AddContainer`, `RemoveContainer`, `GetTopologyHints`, `GetExclusiveCPUs`, `GetCPUAffinity`, `GetAllocatableCPUs`.\n\nTwo policies:\n- **`none`** (`policy_none.go`): No exclusive allocation. Containers share the default CPU set. `GetTopologyHints` returns a single hint with no NUMA affinity, allowing the topology manager to pick any alignment.\n- **`static`** (`policy_static.go`): Guarantees exclusive CPU cores for Guaranteed-QoS pods with integer CPU requests. The static policy maintains `allocatableCPUs` (all CPUs minus reserved system CPUs), `exclusiveCPUs` (pinned to specific containers), and a shared pool. Allocation is logged to a checkpoint file (`cpu_manager_state`) so state survives kubelet restart. A background reconciliation loop (`reconcileState`, period configurable via `--cpu-manager-reconcile-period`) calls `UpdateContainerResources` via CRI to push cpuset changes to already-running containers without restart.\n\nSMT (Hyper-Threading) awareness: the static policy can enforce that exclusive CPU allocations include full physical cores (sibling logical CPUs together), gated by the `CPUManagerPolicyAlignBySocket` and `DistributeCPUsAcrossNUMA` policy options.\n\n---\n\n### 2.8 MemoryManager\n**File:** `pkg/kubelet/cm/memorymanager/`\n\nStructurally parallel to CPUManager. Allocates NUMA memory nodes for Guaranteed-QoS pods, surfaces NUMA hints to the topology manager, and writes its state to a checkpoint file. Its result (NUMA node IDs) is consumed by `PreCreateContainer` to set `cpusetMems`.\n\n---\n\n### 2.9 TopologyManager\n**Files:** `pkg/kubelet/cm/topologymanager/topology_manager.go`, `pkg/kubelet/cm/topologymanager/scope_container.go`, `pkg/kubelet/cm/topologymanager/scope_pod.go`\n\nThe topology manager is a `lifecycle.PodAdmitHandler` (so the kubelet calls `Admit` on every pod before starting it) as well as a `Store` (hint providers query it post-admission for the agreed affinity).\n\n**Hint aggregation**: during `Admit`, the topology manager collects `TopologyHint` maps (resource name → list of NUMA bitmasks, each with a `Preferred` flag) from each registered `HintProvider` (cpuManager, memoryManager, deviceManager). It then computes the cross-product of all hints, merges bitmasks with bitwise AND, and selects the best (most preferred, narrowest NUMA set) result according to the active policy. If no acceptable alignment exists and the policy is `restricted` or `single-numa-node`, the pod is rejected at admission with a `TopologyAffinityError`.\n\n**Policies:**\n| Policy | Behavior |\n|---|---|\n| `none` | Always admits; ignores topology |\n| `best-effort` | Admits regardless; attempts NUMA alignment |\n| `restricted` | Rejects if only non-preferred hints exist |\n| `single-numa-node` | Rejects unless all resources fit in one NUMA node |\n\n**Maximum NUMA nodes**: the topology manager supports at most 8 NUMA nodes (`defaultMaxAllowableNUMANodes = 8`) to bound the hint-cross-product explosion.\n\nAfter `Admit`, hint providers call `GetAffinity(podUID, containerName)` to read the agreed `TopologyHint` and use it to constrain their allocation.\n\n---\n\n### 2.10 DeviceManager\n**Files:** `pkg/kubelet/cm/devicemanager/types.go`, `pkg/kubelet/cm/devicemanager/manager.go`\n\nThe device manager listens on a Unix socket for device plugin gRPC registrations (via the plugin registration handler returned by `GetWatcherHandler`). Each registered plugin is represented as an `endpoint`. The manager:\n- Maintains a checkpoint file (`kubelet_internal_checkpoint`) to persist allocations across kubelet restart.\n- `Allocate(pod, container)` calls the device plugin's `Allocate` gRPC and caches the response (envs, mounts, devices, CDI devices).\n- `GetDeviceRunContainerOptions` returns the cached allocation as `DeviceRunContainerOptions`; this is called from `containerManagerImpl.GetResources`, which merges device options with DRA options before passing them to the container runtime.\n- Stopped endpoints are retained for a 5-minute grace period (`endpointStopGracePeriod`) so that capacity changes propagate before the device is removed from the allocatable pool.\n- Implements `HintProvider` to give the topology manager NUMA locality information about devices.\n\n---\n\n### 2.11 DRA Manager\n**File:** `pkg/kubelet/cm/dra/manager.go`\n\nThe DRA manager handles Dynamic Resource Allocation (feature-gated by `DynamicResourceAllocation`). Unlike device plugins, DRA uses structured `ResourceClaim` objects coordinated with the API server. The manager:\n- `PrepareResources` calls DRA drivers to prepare devices before a pod starts.\n- `UnprepareResources` is called when a pod terminates.\n- `GetResources` provides CDI device names to be passed to the container runtime.\n- `PodMightNeedToUnprepareResources` checks whether cleanup is needed based on checkpoint state.\n- Updates are emitted on a channel and fan-in to `cm.resourceUpdates` if `ResourceHealthStatus` is enabled.\n\n---\n\n## 3. End-to-End Control Flow\n\n### 3.1 Startup\n\n```\nkubelet.go → NewContainerManager() [container_manager_linux.go:207]\n  ├── GetCgroupSubsystems()          // discover mounted cgroup controllers\n  ├── swap.IsSwapOn()                // fail or warn if swap is active\n  ├── cadvisor.MachineInfo()         // node capacity + NUMA topology\n  ├── NewCgroupManager()             // detect v1 vs v2, build path translator\n  ├── NewQOSContainerManager()       // set up Guaranteed/Burstable/BestEffort\n  ├── topologymanager.NewManager()   // NUMA policy + scope\n  ├── devicemanager.NewManagerImpl() // register as topology hint provider\n  ├── dra.NewManager()               // if DynamicResourceAllocation enabled\n  ├── cpumanager.NewManager()        // policy, checkpoint dir, topology hints\n  ├── memorymanager.NewManager()     // policy, checkpoint dir, topology hints\n  └── goroutines: fan-in device/DRA updates → cm.resourceUpdates\n\nContainerManager.Start() [container_manager_linux.go:615]\n  ├── buildContainerMapAndRunningSetFromRuntime()  // bootstrap from CRI state\n  ├── draManager.Start()\n  ├── cpuManager.Start()              // load checkpoint, launch reconciler\n  ├── memoryManager.Start()           // load checkpoint\n  ├── cadvisor.RootFsInfo()           // ephemeral storage capacity\n  ├── validateNodeAllocatable()       // sanity-check reservations ≤ capacity\n  ├── setupNode()\n  │   ├── validateSystemRequirements()  // verify cpu/memory cgroups mounted\n  │   ├── setupKernelTunables()         // vm.overcommit_memory etc.\n  │   ├── createNodeAllocatableCgroups() // /kubepods cgroup\n  │   ├── qosContainerManager.Start()   // create Burstable/BestEffort cgroups\n  │   ├── enforceNodeAllocatableCgroups() // set limits + optional system/kube cgroups\n  │   └── build systemContainers list (system, kubelet process cgroups)\n  ├── goroutine: ensureStateFunc loop (every 1 min) for system containers\n  ├── goroutine: periodicTasks loop (every 5 min) for kubelet cgroup tracking\n  └── deviceManager.Start()           // begin listening for plugin registrations\n```\n\n### 3.2 Pod Admission\n\nThe kubelet calls `ContainerManager.GetAllocateResourcesPodAdmitHandler()`, which returns the topology manager. During admission:\n\n```\ntopologyManager.Admit(pod)\n  ├── for each container:\n  │   ├── cpuManager.GetTopologyHints(pod, container)\n  │   ├── memoryManager.GetTopologyHints(pod, container)\n  │   └── deviceManager.GetTopologyHints(pod, container)\n  ├── aggregate hints → select best NUMA affinity\n  ├── if policy rejects: return Admit=false (TopologyAffinityError)\n  └── store affinity in topologyManager for each (pod, container)\n\ndeviceManager.UpdatePluginResources(nodeInfo, attrs)  // device capacity update\n```\n\nCPUManager allocation happens later in pod sync (not at admission), via `cpuManager.Allocate(pod, container)` called from the kuberuntime.\n\n### 3.3 Container Creation\n\n```\nkuberuntime.startContainer()\n  ├── cm.GetResources(ctx, pod, container)\n  │   ├── draManager.GetResources()   // CDI devices\n  │   └── deviceManager.GetDeviceRunContainerOptions()  // envs, mounts, devices\n  ├── cpuManager.Allocate(pod, container)  // record CPU assignment\n  ├── lifecycle.PreCreateContainer()\n  │   ├── cpuManager.GetCPUAffinity() → containerConfig.CpusetCpus\n  │   └── memoryManager.GetMemoryNUMANodes() → containerConfig.CpusetMems\n  ├── runtimeService.CreateContainer(containerConfig)  // CRI call\n  └── lifecycle.PreStartContainer(containerID)\n      ├── cpuManager.AddContainer(pod, container, containerID)\n      ├── memoryManager.AddContainer(pod, container, containerID)\n      └── topologyManager.AddContainer(pod, container, containerID)\n```\n\n### 3.4 Pod Cgroup Synchronization\n\nDuring every pod sync (`kubelet.go:2048`):\n\n```\npcm = containerManager.NewPodContainerManager()\nif !pcm.Exists(pod):\n    containerManager.UpdateQOSCgroups()   // recompute QoS tier limits\n    pcm.EnsureExists(pod)                 // create /kubepods/<qos>/pod<UID>\n```\n\n`EnsureExists` (`pod_container_manager_linux.go:74`) calls `ResourceConfigForPod` to derive `CgroupConfig` from the pod spec, then delegates to `cgroupManager.Create`.\n\n### 3.5 Container Teardown\n\n```\nlifecycle.PostStopContainer(containerID)\n  └── topologyManager.RemoveContainer(containerID)\n\ncpuManager.RemoveContainer(containerID)    // free exclusive CPUs\nmemoryManager.RemoveContainer(containerID) // free NUMA memory blocks\n\npodContainerManager.Destroy(podCgroupName)\n  ├── tryKillingCgroupProcesses()  // SIGKILL all pids (up to 5 retries)\n  └── cgroupManager.Destroy()      // rmdir cgroup hierarchy\n```\n\n---\n\n## 4. Failure Modes, Limits, and Design Tradeoffs\n\n### 4.1 Failure Modes\n\n| Failure | Behavior |\n|---|---|\n| Required cgroup subsystems not mounted | `NewContainerManager` returns error; kubelet exits |\n| CgroupRoot does not exist | Fatal at startup; kubelet exits |\n| Node allocatable limit exceeds current memory usage | Background goroutine retries cgroup update until evictions reduce usage |\n| Device plugin disconnects | Endpoint marked stopped; kept in cache for 5-minute grace period; capacity zero after grace |\n| CPUManager state file missing or corrupt | Falls back to re-deriving state from CRI; logs reconciliation errors |\n| TopologyManager `restricted` policy; no satisfying NUMA alignment | Pod rejected at admission with `TopologyAffinityError`; visible as pod condition |\n| QoS cgroup update fails | Logged (`Failed to reserve QoS requests`); next minute's goroutine retries |\n| Kernel tunable mismatch with `--protect-kernel-defaults=true` | Fatal at startup |\n\n### 4.2 Limits\n\n- **NUMA nodes**: capped at 8 by `defaultMaxAllowableNUMANodes` (`topology_manager.go:41`). More than 8 NUMA nodes cause the topology manager to refuse to start.\n- **PID limit**: configurable via `--pod-pids-limit`. Applied at the pod cgroup level via `pids.max`.\n- **Cgroup v1 deprecation**: A warning is emitted at startup (`CgroupV1DeprecatedWarning`). The `cpu.stat` file in the cgroup v2 root requires kernel ≥ 5.8; absence triggers a warning.\n- **CPU CFS quota period**: minimum 1 ms (`MinQuotaPeriod`), default 100 ms (`QuotaPeriod`). Shorter periods increase scheduling overhead.\n\n### 4.3 Design Tradeoffs\n\n**Cgroups-per-QoS vs flat:**\nThe `--cgroups-per-qos` flag (default true) creates a three-level cgroup hierarchy. When disabled, `podContainerManagerNoop` is returned and pod cgroup management is skipped entirely. The hierarchy adds 1–3 cgroup directory creations per pod and a periodic reconciliation goroutine, but enables per-QoS resource pooling and correct accounting.\n\n**CPU static policy tradeoffs:**\nThe static policy improves latency predictability by eliminating CPU scheduling noise across pods. The cost is fragmentation: once a container requests 3 exclusive CPUs on a 4-CPU node, only 1 CPU remains in the shared pool. The `none` policy avoids this at the cost of unpredictable scheduling. The policy is persistent across kubelet restart (checkpoint file), but if the checkpoint is lost the kubelet reconciles from CRI state, which can produce temporary duplicate allocations.\n\n**TopologyManager NUMA constraints:**\nSetting `--topology-manager-policy=restricted` or `single-numa-node` can cause pod admission rejections even when the node has enough total resources, because the resources span NUMA nodes. Operators must balance latency improvements (NUMA-local memory bandwidth) against placement flexibility.\n\n**Cgroup v1 vs v2 resource model:**\nCgroup v2 enables `memory.min` (guaranteed minimum memory, prevents lower-priority pressure from evicting committed bytes), and unified `cpu.max` (replaces the period/quota two-file model). The `MemoryQoS` feature gate activates `memory.min` at both QoS and pod cgroup levels. Cgroup v1 has no equivalent of `memory.min`, so `QOSReserved` memory enforcement under v1 is approximate and can fail silently if current usage exceeds the desired limit.\n\n---\n\n## 5. Extension Points and Customization Hooks\n\n### 5.1 CPU Manager Policy\nThe `cpumanager.Policy` interface (`cpu_manager.go`, `policy.go`) is implemented by `none` and `static`. A custom policy can be added by implementing `Policy` (which includes `Allocate`, `AddContainer`, `RemoveContainer`, `GetTopologyHints`, `GetPodTopologyHints`) and registering it in `NewManager`. Policy options (`--cpu-manager-policy-options`) are passed as a string map, allowing feature-flag-style knobs without new interfaces.\n\n**Risk:** Custom policies bypass the standard validation of CPU set coherence (e.g., SMT sibling alignment) unless they replicate those checks. An incorrect policy that double-allocates CPUs can cause silent performance degradation rather than an error.\n\n### 5.2 Topology Manager Policy and Scope\nThe topology manager is constructed with a string policy name and scope name, mapped to implementations via `policy.go` and `scope.go`. Adding a new policy requires implementing the `Policy` interface (two methods: `Name`, `Merge`). The pod-scope vs container-scope choice (set by `--topology-manager-scope`) changes whether hints are aggregated once per pod or once per container.\n\n### 5.3 `HintProvider` Registration\nAny component can register as a `HintProvider` by calling `topologyManager.AddHintProvider(logger, hp)` in `NewContainerManager`. Currently cpuManager, memoryManager, and deviceManager do so. A custom resource manager that understands NUMA locality can hook in here. The topology manager calls `GetTopologyHints` and `GetPodTopologyHints` synchronously during admission; slow hint providers directly delay pod scheduling.\n\n### 5.4 Device Plugin Interface\nDevice plugins register over a gRPC Unix socket in `/var/lib/kubelet/device-plugins/`. The `DeviceManager.GetWatcherHandler()` returns a `cache.PluginHandler` consumed by the plugin registration framework (`pkg/kubelet/pluginmanager`). Plugins must implement the `DevicePlugin` gRPC service from `k8s.io/kubelet/pkg/apis`. CDI device IDs are passed through unmodified to the CRI; the runtime must understand CDI.\n\n### 5.5 DRA Driver Interface\nDRA drivers register via a separate gRPC endpoint (`/var/lib/kubelet/plugins/<driver>/`). The DRA manager's `GetWatcherHandler()` handles driver registration. DRA resources are claimed via `ResourceClaim` Kubernetes objects, not kubelet-local state, making the allocation lifecycle richer but also dependent on API server connectivity.\n\n### 5.6 Kernel Tunable Behavior\n`setupKernelTunables` in `container_manager_linux.go:458` checks six kernel parameters (e.g., `vm.overcommit_memory`, `kernel.panic`). The behavior is controlled by `KernelTunableBehavior`: `warn` (log), `error` (fail startup), or `modify` (set the value). Operators set `--protect-kernel-defaults` to switch from `modify` to `error`. Adding a new tunable requires editing the `desiredState` map, which is not externally extensible without code change.\n\n---\n\n## 6. Operational Implications\n\n### 6.1 Performance\n\n**Periodic reconciliation overhead:** Two goroutines run continuously—QoS cgroup updates (1-minute period, `qos_container_manager_linux.go:141`) and CPUManager reconciliation (configurable, default 10 seconds). Each QoS update reads all active pod specs and calls `cgroupManager.Update` for all three QoS tiers; on nodes with hundreds of pods, this involves multiple cgroup file writes per cycle. The CPUManager reconciler calls `UpdateContainerResources` via CRI for every container that has drifted from its recorded cpuset, which on cgroup v1 involves writing `cpuset.cpus`.\n\n**Cgroup v1 vs v2 write amplification:** Cgroup v1 requires writing each resource limit to a separate file per subsystem. For a pod with CPU and memory limits, `EnsureExists` touches at least `cpu.shares`, `cpu.cfs_quota_us`, `cpu.cfs_period_us`, `memory.limit_in_bytes`, and `pids.max`. Cgroup v2 unifies this into fewer files (`cpu.max`, `memory.max`, `pids.max`) in a single unified hierarchy directory, reducing the number of open/write/close syscalls per pod start.\n\n**Topology hint computation cost:** During pod admission, the topology manager computes the cross-product of all hint combinations. With 3 hint providers and `n` possible NUMA affinities each, the combinatorial cost is O(n³). With 8 NUMA nodes this is bounded, but with many resources per provider (e.g., GPUs on many NUMA nodes), the number of hints per provider can be large.\n\n### 6.2 Reliability\n\n**State persistence:** CPUManager and MemoryManager write checkpoint files (`cpu_manager_state`, `memory_manager_state`) to `--root-dir` on every allocation change. These are the source of truth for resource assignments after a kubelet restart. Loss of these files (e.g., ephemeral root filesystem) forces reconciliation from CRI state, which can leave containers with incorrect cpusets until the next reconcile cycle.\n\n**Orphan cleanup:** `podContainerManager.GetAllPodsFromCgroups` scans the cgroup filesystem at startup to find pod cgroups without corresponding running pods and destroys them. This relies on the `pod<UID>` naming convention in cgroup directory names. If an operator manually creates a directory under a QoS cgroup matching the prefix `pod`, it will be misidentified and deleted.\n\n**Node allocatable enforcement retry:** The goroutine in `enforceNodeAllocatableCgroups` (`node_container_manager_linux.go:95`) retries indefinitely (sleeping 1 minute between attempts) until the memory cgroup update succeeds. During this window, pod memory is not bounded by the allocatable limit. The kubelet emits a warning event (`FailedNodeAllocatableEnforcement`) on each failed attempt, making this observable.\n\n### 6.3 Maintainability\n\n**Platform variance by build tag:** The package uses Go build tags (`//go:build linux`, `//go:build windows`) rather than runtime checks. This means Windows-specific limitations (no cgroups, no QoS hierarchy, DRA unsupported as of the current code with the `TODO` comment in `container_manager_windows.go:289`) are encoded at compile time. Adding a new resource manager requires three files: the shared interface, the Linux implementation, and either a Windows stub or a conditional feature-gated path.\n\n**Feature gate proliferation:** The container manager checks at least eight feature gates (`DynamicResourceAllocation`, `MemoryQoS`, `QOSReserved`, `CPUCFSQuotaPeriod`, `DisableCPUQuotaWithExclusiveCPUs`, `PodLevelResources`, `WindowsCPUAndMemoryAffinity`, `ResourceHealthStatus`, `KubeletInUserNamespace`). Each gate creates a divergent code path that must be tested separately. This is the mechanism for safe feature rollout but increases the surface area of the container manager's behavior space.\n\n---\n\n## 7.
